<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every finite two-player game with entangled value <i>v</i> &lt; 1, we prove exponential decay for the probability of winning at least a $`v+\delta`$ fraction of <i>k</i> independent repetitions, uniformly over all finite-dimensional joint strategies. The result allows arbitrary correlated question distributions and holds for every $`0\lt \delta\lt 1-v`$ and <i>k</i> ≥ 1. Its universal rate is proportional to $`\delta^5/(1+\log(|\mathcal A||\mathcal B|))`$, where $`\mathcal A,\mathcal B`$ are the answer alphabets. For each fixed question distribution, we also obtain an explicit cubic rate in <i>δ</i>.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a universal constant $\kappa_0>0$ such that the following holds. Let $G$ be any finite two-player one-round game with Boolean acceptance predicate and entangled value $v=\omega^*(G)<1$. For every $0<\delta<1-v$, every integer $k\ge1$, and every finite-dimensional joint strategy for $k$ repetitions, $$\label{eq:main} \Pr\!\left[W_k\ge\lceil(v+\delta)k\rceil\right] \le \exp\!\left(-\frac{\kappa_0\delta^5} {1+\log d}\,k\right).$$ The same inequality holds for the supremum over those strategies.
  - Explanation: Bounds threshold wins exponentially for arbitrary correlated question distributions and finite-dimensional joint strategies.
- **C2** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mean-bound)): Let $G$ be a finite two-prover game with entangled value $v$, and let $I_1,\ldots,I_k$ be the coordinate win indicators of any finite-dimensional strategy for its $k$-fold repetition. Then $$\mathbb E I_i\le v\quad(1\le i\le k), \qquad \mathbb E\sum_{i=1}^k I_i\le kv.$$ In particular, if $v=0$, then every coordinate is lost almost surely.
  - Explanation: Bounds every coordinate marginal by the single-game value and settles the zero-value case.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tail-sampling)): Let $I_1,\ldots,I_k$ be arbitrary jointly distributed $\{0,1\}$-valued random variables. Suppose $0<q_0<q<1$, $0<\varepsilon<1$, and $p_H=\Pr(W\ge\lceil qk\rceil)>0$. Put $$\ell=\left\lceil \frac{\log(4/(\varepsilon p_H))}{\log(q/q_0)} \right\rceil.$$ There is a set $C\subseteq[k]$, with $\|C\|\le\ell$, such that $$\label{eq:sampled-set-guarantees} p_C\ge\tfrac12p_Hq^\ell, \qquad \Pr(W<q_0k\mid E_C)\le\varepsilon.$$
  - Explanation: Selects a tested set from an upper tail without independence assumptions on the win indicators.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-conditioning-to-tail)): Let $G$ be a finite two-prover game with nonempty answer alphabets, $d=\|\mathcal A\|\|\mathcal B\|$, and $v=\omega^*(G)<1$. Fix $0<\delta<1-v$ and $0<T\le\delta/4$. Suppose the following holds for every $k\ge1$, every finite-dimensional strategy for $k$ repetitions, and every $C\subsetneq[k]$ with $p_C>0$: $$\label{eq:threshold-input} \frac{\log(1/p_C)+\|C\|\log d}{k-\|C\|}\le T \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \frac{1}{k-\|C\|}\sum_{i\notin C}\Pr(I_i=1\mid E_C) \le v+\delta/4.$$ Then, for every $k\ge1$ and every finite-dimensional strategy, $$\label{eq:generic-threshold-rate} \Pr\bigl(W\ge\lceil(v+\delta)k\rceil\bigr) \le \exp\left(-\frac{\delta T}{16(1+\log d)}\,k\right).$$ The same bound holds for the supremum over those strategies.
  - Explanation: Transfers a uniform conditioning budget into exponential threshold decay at every repetition length.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-song-conditioning)): There is a universal constant $B_4\ge1$ with the following property. Let $G$ be a finite two-player game with entangled value $v<1$, and fix a finite-dimensional strategy for $k\ge1$ repetitions. For $C\subsetneq[k]$ with $p_C=\Pr(E_C)>0$, put $m=k-\|C\|$ and $$\tau=\frac{\log(1/p_C)+\|C\|\log(\|\mathcal A\|\|\mathcal B\|)}m.$$ If $\tau\le1$, then $$\label{eq:song-conditioning} \frac1m\sum_{i\notin C}\Pr(I_i=1\mid E_C) \le v+B_4\tau^{1/4}.$$
  - Explanation: Imports a universal fourth-root conditioning estimate from the cited external theorem.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-resolvent)): For every effect $T$ on $\mathcal H$, $\mathcal F_T^*\mathcal F_T=T$. If $A_1,\ldots,A_N$ are effects, $q_1,\ldots,q_N>0$ sum to $1$, and $B=\sum_jq_jA_j$, then $$\sum_j q_j(\mathcal F_{A_j}-\mathcal F_B)^* (\mathcal F_{A_j}-\mathcal F_B) \preceq \sum_jq_j f(A_j)-f(B). \label{eq:resolvent-jensen}$$ For any finite collection $\mathscr T$ of effects, all maps $\mathcal F_T$, $T\in\mathscr T$, can instead take values in one finite-dimensional space, preserving every product $\mathcal F_S^*\mathcal F_T$, $S,T\in\mathscr T$.
  - Explanation: Constructs compatible purifications and bounds their averaged squared distances by an operator entropy gap.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-measurement-transport)): Let $\mathcal H,\mathcal K$ be finite-dimensional complex Hilbert spaces, let $T$ be an effect on $\mathcal H$, and let $F:\mathcal H\to\mathcal K$ satisfy $F^*F=T$. Suppose that $\mathcal A$ is a nonempty finite set and that positive operators $(G_a)_{a\in\mathcal A}$ satisfy $\sum_aG_a=T$. There is a POVM $(\widetilde G_a)_{a\in\mathcal A}$ on $\mathcal K$ with $$F^*\widetilde G_aF=G_a\qquad(a\in\mathcal A). \label{eq:measurement-pullback}$$ The POVM depends only on $F$ and the operators $G_a$.
  - Explanation: Transports answer measurements while preserving their pullbacks exactly.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exposure)): For any finite-dimensional repeated-game strategy, proper tested set $C$ with $p=\Pr(E_C)>0$, and fixed ordering of the $m=k-\|C\|$ untested coordinates, the preceding construction gives finite-dimensional vectors and local POVMs satisfying the exact conditional answer law <a href="#eq:exposure-fine-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exposure-fine-law">[eq:exposure-fine-law]</a>, transcript law <a href="#eq:exposure-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exposure-law">[eq:exposure-law]</a>, and norm identities <a href="#eq:exposure-norms" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exposure-norms">[eq:exposure-norms]</a>. With $\tau=(\|C\|\log(\|\mathcal A\|\|\mathcal B\|)+\log(1/p))/m$, $$\label{eq:exposure-errors} \mathbb E_i e_A(i)\le p\tau,\qquad \mathbb E_i e_B(i)\le p\tau,\qquad \mathbb E_i\bigl(e_A(i)+e_B(i)\bigr)\le2p\tau,$$ where $i$ is uniform on $[m]$ and the errors are defined in <a href="#eq:mode-error-definitions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mode-error-definitions">[eq:mode-error-definitions]</a>. The question distribution may have zero entries and disconnected support.
  - Explanation: Constructs exact conditional answer laws and controls local-description errors by entropy telescoping.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-question-drift)): Under the preceding assumptions, put $$\Delta_i=\\|\operatorname{law}(X_i,Y_i\mid E_C)-\mu\\|_1.$$ For a uniformly chosen untested coordinate, $$\mathbb E_i\Delta_i \le\sqrt{\frac{2\log(1/p)}{m}}\le\sqrt{2\tau}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the average full ℓ₁ change in the question distribution after conditioning.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-root-conditioning)): Let $G$ be a finite two-prover game with question distribution $\mu$ and $v=\omega^*(G)$. For any finite-dimensional strategy for $k$ copies and $C\subsetneq[k]$, suppose that $$E_C=\{I_j=1\text{ for every }j\in C\},\qquad p=\Pr(E_C)>0.$$ Put $m=k-\|C\|$ and $\tau=(\|C\|\log(\|\mathcal A\|\|\mathcal B\|)+\log(1/p))/m$. Then $$\label{eq:root-conditioning} \frac1m\sum_{i\notin C}\Pr(I_i=1\mid E_C) \le v+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/p)}{2m}} +4\sqrt{\kappa_\mu\tau}+2\kappa_\mu\tau.$$ The constants are independent of the strategy dimension and of $k$.
  - Explanation: Bounds conditional success using reference vectors within connected support components.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-support-threshold)): Let $G$ be a finite two-player game with Boolean acceptance predicate, question distribution $\mu$, and entangled value $v<1$. Put $d=\|\mathcal A\|\|\mathcal B\|$, and define $\kappa_\mu$ by <a href="#eq:root-support-constant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:root-support-constant">[eq:root-support-constant]</a>. For every $0<\delta<1-v$, every $k\ge1$, and every finite-dimensional joint strategy for $k$ repetitions, $$\label{eq:support-threshold-rate} \Pr\bigl(W_k\ge\lceil(v+\delta)k\rceil\bigr) \le\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^3} {16384(1+\kappa_\mu)(1+\log d)}\,k\right).$$ The same bound holds for the supremum over those strategies.
  - Explanation: Gives a cubic threshold rate whose constant depends on the fixed question distribution.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-error-amplification)): Let $G$ be a finite two-player one-round game in the model above, and fix $0\le s<c\le1$. Put $\Delta=c-s$, $t=(c+s)/2$, and $d=\|\mathcal A\|\|\mathcal B\|$. For an integer $k\ge1$, let $H_k$ sample $k$ independent question pairs from $G$, send each player the entire local question string, and accept exactly when $W_k\ge\lceil tk\rceil$. Then $$\begin{aligned} \omega^*(G)\ge c &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \omega^*(H_k)\ge1-\exp(-\Delta^2k/8),\\ \omega^*(G)\le s &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \omega^*(H_k)\le \exp\!\left(-\frac{\kappa_0(\Delta/2)^5}{1+\log d}\,k\right), \end{aligned}$$ where $\kappa_0$ is the universal constant in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.
  - Explanation: Amplifies completeness and soundness using a threshold between their promised values.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-tail-sampling",
        "exact_quote": "Let $I_1,\\ldots,I_k$ be arbitrary jointly distributed $\\{0,1\\}$-valued random variables. Suppose $0<q_0<q<1$, $0<\\varepsilon<1$, and $p_H=\\Pr(W\\ge\\lceil qk\\rceil)>0$. Put $$\\ell=\\left\\lceil\n \\frac{\\log(4/(\\varepsilon p_H))}{\\log(q/q_0)}\n \\right\\rceil.$$ There is a set $C\\subseteq[k]$, with $|C|\\le\\ell$, such that $$\\label{eq:sampled-set-guarantees}\n p_C\\ge\\tfrac12p_Hq^\\ell,\n \\qquad \\Pr(W<q_0k\\mid E_C)\\le\\varepsilon.$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-277.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-conditioning-to-tail",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite two-prover game with nonempty answer alphabets, $d=|\\mathcal A||\\mathcal B|$, and $v=\\omega^*(G)<1$. Fix $0<\\delta<1-v$ and $0<T\\le\\delta/4$. Suppose the following holds for every $k\\ge1$, every finite-dimensional strategy for $k$ repetitions, and every $C\\subsetneq[k]$ with $p_C>0$: $$\\label{eq:threshold-input}\n \\frac{\\log(1/p_C)+|C|\\log d}{k-|C|}\\le T\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\frac{1}{k-|C|}\\sum_{i\\notin C}\\Pr(I_i=1\\mid E_C)\n \\le v+\\delta/4.$$ Then, for every $k\\ge1$ and every finite-dimensional strategy, $$\\label{eq:generic-threshold-rate}\n \\Pr\\bigl(W\\ge\\lceil(v+\\delta)k\\rceil\\bigr)\n \\le \\exp\\left(-\\frac{\\delta T}{16(1+\\log d)}\\,k\\right).$$ The same bound holds for the supremum over those strategies.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-277.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-conditioning-to-tail",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite two-prover game with nonempty answer alphabets, $d=|\\mathcal A||\\mathcal B|$, and $v=\\omega^*(G)<1$. Fix $0<\\delta<1-v$ and $0<T\\le\\delta/4$. Suppose the following holds for every $k\\ge1$, every finite-dimensional strategy for $k$ repetitions, and every $C\\subsetneq[k]$ with $p_C>0$: $$\\label{eq:threshold-input}\n \\frac{\\log(1/p_C)+|C|\\log d}{k-|C|}\\le T\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\frac{1}{k-|C|}\\sum_{i\\notin C}\\Pr(I_i=1\\mid E_C)\n \\le v+\\delta/4.$$ Then, for every $k\\ge1$ and every finite-dimensional strategy, $$\\label{eq:generic-threshold-rate}\n \\Pr\\bigl(W\\ge\\lceil(v+\\delta)k\\rceil\\bigr)\n \\le \\exp\\left(-\\frac{\\delta T}{16(1+\\log d)}\\,k\\right).$$ The same bound holds for the supremum over those strategies.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-277.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G$ be a finite two-prover game with nonempty answer alphabets, $d=|\\mathcal A||\\mathcal B|$, and $v=\\omega^*(G)<1$. Fix $0<\\delta<1-v$ and $0<T\\le\\delta/4$. Suppose the following holds for every $k\\ge1$, every finite-dimensional strategy for $k$ repetitions, and every $C\\subsetneq[k]$ with $p_C>0$: $$\\label{eq:threshold-input}\n \\frac{\\log(1/p_C)+|C|\\log d}{k-|C|}\\le T\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\frac{1}{k-|C|}\\sum_{i\\notin C}\\Pr(I_i=1\\mid E_C)\n \\le v+\\delta/4.$$ Then, for every $k\\ge1$ and every finite-dimensional strategy, $$\\label{eq:generic-threshold-rate}\n \\Pr\\bigl(W\\ge\\lceil(v+\\delta)k\\rceil\\bigr)\n \\le \\exp\\left(-\\frac{\\delta T}{16(1+\\log d)}\\,k\\right).$$ The same bound holds for the supremum over those strategies.",

Suppose the following holds for every $k\\ge1$, every finite-dimensional strategy for $k$ repetitions, and every $C\\subsetneq[k]$ with $p_C>0$: $$\\label{eq:threshold-input}\n \\frac{\\log(1/p_C)+|C|\\log d}{k-|C|}\\le T\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\frac{1}{k-|C|}\\sum_{i\\notin C}\\Pr(I_i=1\\mid E_C)\n \\le v+\\delta/4.$$ Then, for every $k\\ge1$ and every finite-dimensional strategy, $$\\label{eq:generic-threshold-rate}\n \\Pr\\bigl(W\\ge\\lceil(v+\\delta)k\\rceil\\bigr)\n \\le \\exp\\left(-\\frac{\\delta T}{16(1+\\log d)}\\,k\\right).$$ The same bound holds for the supremum over those strategies.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-song-conditioning",
        "exact_quote": "There is a universal constant $B_4\\ge1$ with the following property. Let $G$ be a finite two-player game with entangled value $v<1$, and fix a finite-dimensional strategy for $k\\ge1$ repetitions. For $C\\subsetneq[k]$ with $p_C=\\Pr(E_C)>0$, put $m=k-|C|$ and $$\\tau=\\frac{\\log(1/p_C)+|C|\\log(|\\mathcal A||\\mathcal B|)}m.$$ If $\\tau\\le1$, then $$\\label{eq:song-conditioning}\n \\frac1m\\sum_{i\\notin C}\\Pr(I_i=1\\mid E_C)\n \\le v+B_4\\tau^{1/4}.$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-277.1"
      },

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-277.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-resolvent",
        "exact_quote": "For every effect $T$ on $\\mathcal H$, $\\mathcal F_T^*\\mathcal F_T=T$. If $A_1,\\ldots,A_N$ are effects, $q_1,\\ldots,q_N>0$ sum to $1$, and $B=\\sum_jq_jA_j$, then $$\\sum_j q_j(\\mathcal F_{A_j}-\\mathcal F_B)^*\n                 (\\mathcal F_{A_j}-\\mathcal F_B)\n \\preceq \\sum_jq_j f(A_j)-f(B).\n \\label{eq:resolvent-jensen}$$ For any finite collection $\\mathscr T$ of effects, all maps $\\mathcal F_T$, $T\\in\\mathscr T$, can instead take values in one finite-dimensional space, preserving every product $\\mathcal F_S^*\\mathcal F_T$, $S,T\\in\\mathscr T$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-277.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-resolvent",
          "exact_quote": "For every effect $T$ on $\\mathcal H$, $\\mathcal F_T^*\\mathcal F_T=T$.           "source_id": "canonical:PM-277.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every effect $T$ on $\\mathcal H$, $\\mathcal F_T^*\\mathcal F_T=T$.       "explanation": "Constructs compatible purifications and bounds their averaged squared distances by an operator entropy gap.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-measurement-transport",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal H,\\mathcal K$ be finite-dimensional complex Hilbert spaces, let $T$ be an effect on $\\mathcal H$, and let $F:\\mathcal H\\to\\mathcal K$ satisfy $F^*F=T$. Suppose that $\\mathcal A$ is a nonempty finite set and that positive operators $(G_a)_{a\\in\\mathcal A}$ satisfy $\\sum_aG_a=T$. There is a POVM $(\\widetilde G_a)_{a\\in\\mathcal A}$ on $\\mathcal K$ with $$F^*\\widetilde G_aF=G_a\\qquad(a\\in\\mathcal A).\n \\label{eq:measurement-pullback}$$ The POVM depends only on $F$ and the operators $G_a$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-277.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-measurement-transport",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal H,\\mathcal K$ be finite-dimensional complex Hilbert spaces, let $T$ be an effect on $\\mathcal H$, and let $F:\\mathcal H\\to\\mathcal K$ satisfy $F^*F=T$. There is a POVM $(\\widetilde G_a)_{a\\in\\mathcal A}$ on $\\mathcal K$ with $$F^*\\widetilde G_aF=G_a\\qquad(a\\in\\mathcal A).\n \\label{eq:measurement-pullback}$$ The POVM depends only on $F$ and the operators $G_a$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-277.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mathcal H,\\mathcal K$ be finite-dimensional complex Hilbert spaces, let $T$ be an effect on $\\mathcal H$, and let $F:\\mathcal H\\to\\mathcal K$ satisfy $F^*F=T$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-exposure",
        "exact_quote": "For any finite-dimensional repeated-game strategy, proper tested set $C$ with $p=\\Pr(E_C)>0$, and fixed ordering of the $m=k-|C|$ untested coordinates, the preceding construction gives finite-dimensional vectors and local POVMs satisfying the exact conditional answer law <a href=\"#eq:exposure-fine-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exposure-fine-law\">[eq:exposure-fine-law]</a>, transcript law <a href=\"#eq:exposure-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exposure-law\">[eq:exposure-law]</a>, and norm identities <a href=\"#eq:exposure-norms\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exposure-norms\">[eq:exposure-norms]</a>. With $\\tau=(|C|\\log(|\\mathcal A||\\mathcal B|)+\\log(1/p))/m$, $$\\label{eq:exposure-errors}\n \\mathbb E_i e_A(i)\\le p\\tau,\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_i e_B(i)\\le p\\tau,\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_i\\bigl(e_A(i)+e_B(i)\\bigr)\\le2p\\tau,$$ where $i$ is uniform on $[m]$ and the errors are defined in <a href=\"#eq:mode-error-definitions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mode-error-definitions\">[eq:mode-error-definitions]</a>. The question distribution may have zero entries and disconnected support.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-277.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-exposure",
          "exact_quote": "For any finite-dimensional repeated-game strategy, proper tested set $C$ with $p=\\Pr(E_C)>0$, and fixed ordering of the $m=k-|C|$ untested coordinates, the preceding construction gives finite-dimensional vectors and local POVMs satisfying the exact conditional answer law <a href=\"#eq:exposure-fine-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exposure-fine-law\">[eq:exposure-fine-law]</a>, transcript law <a href=\"#eq:exposure-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exposure-law\">[eq:exposure-law]</a>, and norm identities <a href=\"#eq:exposure-norms\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exposure-norms\">[eq:exposure-norms]</a>. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Finite two-player one-round game** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A finite two-player one-round game consists of nonempty finite question sets $\mathcal X,\mathcal Y$, nonempty finite answer sets $\mathcal A,\mathcal B$, a probability distribution $\mu$ on $\mathcal X\times\mathcal Y$, and a predicate $$V:\mathcal X\times\mathcal Y\times\mathcal A\times\mathcal B \longrightarrow\{0,1\}.$$
- **Finite-dimensional entangled strategy** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A finite-dimensional entangled strategy consists of a unit vector $\psi\in\mathcal H_A\otimes\mathcal H_B$ and, for each local question, a positive operator-valued measurement (POVM): positive operators $M_x^a,N_y^b$ satisfying $\sum_aM_x^a=I$ and $\sum_bN_y^b=I$.
- **Entangled value** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The entangled value $\omega^*(G)$ is the supremum of this expression over all finite dimensions and all such strategies; the supremum need not be attained.
- **Win count and answer alphabet product** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $$I_i=V(X_i,Y_i,A_i,B_i),\qquad W_k=\sum_{i=1}^k I_i, \qquad d=\|\mathcal A\|\|\mathcal B\|.$$
- **Conditioning cost** ([source](#sec:threshold)): For $C\subsetneq[k]$ with $p_C>0$, define its conditioning cost by $$\tau(C)=\frac{\log(1/p_C)+\|C\|\log d}{k-\|C\|}.$$
- **Effect** ([source](#sec:operators)): For a finite-dimensional complex Hilbert space $\mathcal H$, an *effect* is a self-adjoint operator $T$ with $0\preceq T\preceq I$.
- **Resolvent purification** ([source](#sec:operators)): Put $f(t)=t\log t$ for $t>0$ and $f(0)=0$, and define $$\mathcal F_T:\mathcal H\longrightarrow L^2((0,\infty),ds;\mathcal H),\qquad (\mathcal F_T h)(s)=T(T+sI)^{-1}h.$$
- **Support component constant** ([source](#eq:root-support-constant)): Let $H_j$ be the connected components, and define $$\label{eq:root-support-constant} \lambda_j=\mu(H_j),\qquad \mu_j=\mu(\,\cdot\mid H_j),\qquad \mu_{j,\min}=\min_{z\in H_j}\mu_j(z),\qquad \kappa_\mu=8\max_j\frac{\|H_j\|-1}{\mu_{j,\min}}.$$
- **Amplified game** ([source](#stmt-cor-error-amplification)): For an integer $k\ge1$, let $H_k$ sample $k$ independent question pairs from $G$, send each player the entire local question string, and accept exactly when $W_k\ge\lceil tk\rceil$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-mean-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mean-bound): Freeze the other questions and average single-game local strategies. -> Bounds every coordinate marginal by the single-game value and settles the zero-value case.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-tail-sampling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tail-sampling): Sample indices with replacement and retain draws with sufficient good-event weight. -> Selects a tested set from an upper tail without independence assumptions on the win indicators.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-conditioning-to-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-conditioning-to-tail): Contradict the conditioning bound for large lengths, then take independent tensor copies. -> Transfers a uniform conditioning budget into exponential threshold decay at every repetition length.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-song-conditioning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-song-conditioning): Apply the cited external conditioning theorem. -> Imports a universal fourth-root conditioning estimate from the cited external theorem.
- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-song-conditioning): Choose the fourth-power budget and apply the conditioning-to-threshold transfer. -> Bounds threshold wins exponentially for arbitrary correlated question distributions and finite-dimensional joint strategies.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-resolvent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-resolvent): Use the scalar resolvent identity, operator comparison, and common-span compression. -> Constructs compatible purifications and bounds their averaged squared distances by an operator entropy gap.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-measurement-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-measurement-transport): Use support inverses and complete the transported effects on the orthogonal complement. -> Transports answer measurements while preserving their pullbacks exactly.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-lem-exposure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exposure): Construct mixed exposure flags and telescope operator entropy gaps. -> Constructs exact conditional answer laws and controls local-description errors by entropy telescoping.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-lem-question-drift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-question-drift): Bound relative entropy to the product law and apply Pinsker and Cauchy–Schwarz. -> Bounds the average full ℓ₁ change in the question distribution after conditioning.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-prop-root-conditioning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-root-conditioning): Replace vectors along support paths and compare conditional component weights. -> Bounds conditional success using reference vectors within connected support components.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-cor-support-threshold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-support-threshold): Choose a quadratic conditioning budget and apply the transfer. -> Gives a cubic threshold rate whose constant depends on the fixed question distribution.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-cor-error-amplification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-error-amplification): Use tensor copies and Hoeffding for completeness and the threshold theorem for soundness. -> Amplifies completeness and soundness using a threshold between their promised values.

For every $0<\\delta<1-v$, every $k\\ge1$, and every finite-dimensional joint strategy for $k$ repetitions, $$\\label{eq:support-threshold-rate}\n \\Pr\\bigl(W_k\\ge\\lceil(v+\\delta)k\\rceil\\bigr)\n \\le\\exp\\left(-\\frac{\\delta^3}\n {16384(1+\\kappa_\\mu)(1+\\log d)}\\,k\\right).$$ The same bound holds for the supremum over those strategies.",

        }

For an integer $k\\ge1$, let $H_k$ sample $k$ independent question pairs from $G$, send each player the entire local question string, and accept exactly when $W_k\\ge\\lceil tk\\rceil$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\omega^*(G)\\ge c\n &\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\omega^*(H_k)\\ge1-\\exp(-\\Delta^2k/8),\\\\\n \\omega^*(G)\\le s\n &\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\omega^*(H_k)\\le\n \\exp\\!\\left(-\\frac{\\kappa_0(\\Delta/2)^5}{1+\\log d}\\,k\\right),\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $\\kappa_0$ is the universal constant in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I1**: The threshold bound is uniform over finite-dimensional joint strategies, whose coordinate wins may be correlated.
- **I2**: Independent question-pair sampling is required across repetitions; independence of coordinate wins is not required.
- **I3**: The support constant uses the minimum component-conditional question mass; singleton components contribute zero.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A1**: Apply the tail bound to retries with dependent question draws. -> The repeated question law does not meet the independent sampling premise.
- **A2**: Treat communicating agents as the game players. -> Communication can invalidate the single-game entangled-value bound.
- **A3**: Interpret the support-constant computation as certification of the threshold theorem. -> The finite graph computation does not verify the analytic conditioning estimates or bound the entangled value.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite support graph explicitly constructs the distribution-dependent constant. The code computes that constant for rational question distributions; it does not certify the entangled value or the analytic threshold inequalities.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON batch of nonempty rectangular matrices of rational strings.
# PRECONDITIONS: Nonnegative masses with positive total; normalize to a probability distribution.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of reduced rational strings for the support-component constant.
# TIME: O(sum(N+n^2)) rational operations, N matrix cells and n positive support pairs.
# SPACE: O(max(N+n)) working storage apart from the input and output batch.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact arbitrary-precision rational arithmetic using fmpq.
# FAILURE: Invalid matrices or masses raise ValueError; no approximate fallback.
import json
import sys
from flint import fmpq

def constant(rows):
    if not rows or not rows[0] or any(len(r) != len(rows[0]) for r in rows):
        raise ValueError('nonempty rectangular matrix required')
    mass = {(x,y): fmpq(s) for x,r in enumerate(rows) for y,s in enumerate(r)}
    if any(w < 0 for w in mass.values()) or sum(mass.values(), fmpq(0)) <= 0:
        raise ValueError('nonnegative positive-total masses required')
    unseen = {z for z,w in mass.items() if w > 0}
    best = fmpq(0)
    while unseen:
        root = min(unseen)
        unseen.remove(root)
        component = {root}
        stack = [root]
        while stack:
            x,y = stack.pop()
            neighbors = {z for z in unseen if z[0] == x or z[1] == y}
            unseen.difference_update(neighbors)
            component.update(neighbors)
            stack.extend(sorted(neighbors))
        total = sum((mass[z] for z in component), fmpq(0))
        minimum = min(mass[z] / total for z in component)
        value = 8 * (len(component) - 1) / minimum
        best = max(best, value)
    return str(best)

print(json.dumps([constant(rows) for rows in json.load(sys.stdin)]))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The stated verifier-family consequence supports adapting game-based verification software to threshold repetition when its model and completeness and soundness promises are established. It supplies an amplification contract, not a general software reliability guarantee. |
| games_simulation | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The paper directly supplies threshold-tail and two-sided amplification guarantees for the specified repeated nonlocal games. A simulator may explore a specified strategy, while the analytic guarantee covers arbitrary finite-dimensional joint strategies. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper develops analytic conditioning and purification arguments but does not supply a standalone software tool, interface, or deployment contract. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The tested-coordinate argument supplies a probabilistic selection construction for arbitrary jointly distributed binary win indicators. Its use is adaptable only with access to the joint law or the required probabilities; the statement establishes existence rather than an efficient general-purpose search procedure. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Support components and transcripts organize the mathematical proof; the source gives no data-structure operation contract or storage complexity result. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite-dimensional states and POVMs specify a mathematical strategy model, without an accelerator architecture, resource bound, or physical implementation contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The soundness theorem controls threshold acceptance in the stated game model. No cryptographic primitive, secrecy guarantee, or adversary model beyond that game is established. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source analyzes repeated game acceptance and entangled strategies, without a learning objective, training procedure, or generalization contract. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The supremum defining entangled value is a theoretical benchmark; the source gives no optimisation solver or finite search bound for obtaining it. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The model requires non-communicating players and a referee sampling independent question pairs; it supplies no consensus, replication, or message-passing correctness contract. |
| other (quantum_information_and_computational_complexity) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | Quantum information and computational complexity are direct applications: the theorem controls joint entangled strategies, and threshold repetition amplifies game-based verifier completeness and soundness. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The game model requires non-communicating players, finite question and answer sets, and Boolean acceptance. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to communicating agents or an unestablished game reduction.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): Question pairs are sampled independently across repetitions; coordinate wins may remain correlated under a joint strategy. Blocked use: ['Replacing independent question sampling by adaptive sampling.', 'Treating joint-strategy wins as independent Bernoulli trials.']
- **L3** (formal_scope): The value and repetition bounds concern the supremum over finite-dimensional entangled strategies; attainment is not required. Blocked use: ['Claiming an optimal strategy exists.', 'Extending the theorem to unrestricted infinite-dimensional models without another argument.']
- **L4** (hypothesis): Threshold decay requires $v<1$ and $0<\delta<1-v$; the integer ceiling is part of the acceptance event. Blocked use: ['Using a threshold at or below the entangled value.', 'Applying the displayed rate outside the strict gap regime.']
- **L5** (edge_case): When $v=0$, every coordinate is lost almost surely, giving the sharper zero tail probability. Blocked use: ['Reporting only a positive exponential upper bound as the sharp zero-value conclusion.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): Conditioning estimates require a proper tested set and strictly positive conditioning-event probability. Blocked use: ['Conditioning on a zero-probability tested event.', 'Dividing by the number of untested coordinates when none remain.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): The universal conditioning estimate is invoked only in its stated conditioning-budget regime and is imported as an external theorem. Blocked use: ['Treating the imported estimate as independently proved here.', 'Using its fourth-root estimate outside the stated regime.']
- **L8** (nonconstructive): The universal constant is specified symbolically through the external conditioning constant, without a numerical calibration. Blocked use: ['Publishing a numerical universal repetition count without an established constant.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): The fixed-distribution rate depends on positive question masses as well as support geometry; it is not a distribution-uniform cubic guarantee. Blocked use: ['Dropping the support constant from the cubic rate.', 'Assuming stability when positive support masses become arbitrarily small.']
- **L10** (formal_scope): The transcript is an analysis variable, not extra information freely supplied to the players. Blocked use: ['Building a strategy whose state or shared transcript depends on both fresh questions.']
- **L11** (nonconstructive): Common finite-dimensional compression preserves the required inner products but gives no uniform resource bound. Blocked use: ['Interpreting dimension-independent decay as a bound on implementation dimension or memory.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): The polynomial-time verifier consequence requires efficiently computable rational completeness and soundness bounds, inverse-polynomial gap, and polynomial answer bit-length. Blocked use: ['Transferring error amplification to arbitrary software retries or cryptographic security without establishing the game premises.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 277 (`lean/docs/277.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Threshold parallel repetition for finite-dimensional entangled games](../../preprints/Threshold-parallel-repetition-for-finite-dimensional-entangled-games-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization gives exponential threshold parallel repetition for every finite two-player game with arbitrary correlated questions. If its finite-dimensional entangled value is $v<1$, its answer sets are $A,B$, and $0<\delta<1-v$, then the probability of winning at least the fraction $v+\delta$ of $k$ repetitions is at most $\exp(-\kappa\delta^{13}k/(1+\log(|A||B|)))$ for one universal $\kappa>0$. The bound applies to every joint finite-dimensional strategy and to their supremum, without assuming attainment. The separate distribution-dependent cubic bound is outside this scope.

Comparator source `EntangledGames.lean` (3180 bytes, sha256 `b3957e18f10af74244e7a2fc1b6cf12b14c9dc4b036f7ffe2ef2b2810e6697c0`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators ComplexOrder

namespace ThresholdParallelRepetition

structure Game (x y a b : ℕ) where
  questionProb : Fin (x + 1) × Fin (y + 1) → ℝ
  questionProb_nonneg : ∀ z, 0 ≤ questionProb z
  questionProb_sum : ∑ z, questionProb z = 1
  accepts : Fin (x + 1) → Fin (y + 1) → Fin (a + 1) → Fin (b + 1) → Bool

structure Strategy (X Y A B : Type) [Fintype A] [Fintype B] where
  dimA : ℕ
  dimB : ℕ
  state : Fin (dimA + 1) × Fin (dimB + 1) → ℂ
  state_unit : ∑ i, Complex.normSq (state i) = 1
  alice : X → A → Matrix (Fin (dimA + 1)) (Fin (dimA + 1)) ℂ
  bob : Y → B → Matrix (Fin (dimB + 1)) (Fin (dimB + 1)) ℂ
  alice_pos : ∀ x a, (alice x a).PosSemidef
  bob_pos : ∀ y b, (bob y b).PosSemidef
  alice_total : ∀ x, ∑ a, alice x a = 1
  bob_total : ∀ y, ∑ b, bob y b = 1

def Strategy.born {X Y A B : Type} [Fintype A] [Fintype B]
    (S : Strategy X Y A B) (x : X) (y : Y) (a : A) (b : B) : ℝ :=
  ∑ i, ∑ j, (star (S.state i) *
    (S.alice x a i.1 j.1 * S.bob y b i.2 j.2) * S.state j).re

abbrev SingleStrategy (x y a b : ℕ) :=
  Strategy (Fin (x + 1)) (Fin (y + 1)) (Fin (a + 1)) (Fin (b + 1))

def successProbability {x y a b : ℕ} (G : Game x y a b) (S : SingleStrategy x y a b) : ℝ :=
  ∑ z : Fin (x + 1) × Fin (y + 1), G.questionProb z *
    ∑ w : Fin (a + 1) × Fin (b + 1),
      if G.accepts z.1 z.2 w.1 w.2 then S.born z.1 z.2 w.1 w.2 else 0

def entangledValue {x y a b : ℕ} (G : Game x y a b) : ℝ :=
  sSup (Set.range (successProbability G))

abbrev RepeatedStrategy (x y a b k : ℕ) :=
  Strategy (Fin k → Fin (x + 1)) (Fin k → Fin (y + 1))
    (Fin k → Fin (a + 1)) (Fin k → Fin (b + 1))

def wins {x y a b k : ℕ} (G : Game x y a b)
    (xs : Fin k → Fin (x + 1)) (ys : Fin k → Fin (y + 1))
    (as : Fin k → Fin (a + 1)) (bs : Fin k → Fin (b + 1)) : ℕ :=
  ∑ i, if G.accepts (xs i) (ys i) (as i) (bs i) then 1 else 0

def thresholdProbability {x y a b k : ℕ} (G : Game x y a b) (δ : ℝ)
    (S : RepeatedStrategy x y a b k) : ℝ :=
  ∑ xs : Fin k → Fin (x + 1), ∑ ys : Fin k → Fin (y + 1),
    (∏ i, G.questionProb (xs i, ys i)) *
      ∑ as : Fin k → Fin (a + 1), ∑ bs : Fin k → Fin (b + 1),
        if ⌈(entangledValue G + δ) * (k : ℝ)⌉₊ ≤ wins G xs ys as bs
        then S.born xs ys as bs else 0

def thresholdValue {x y a b k : ℕ} (G : Game x y a b) (δ : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  sSup (Set.range (thresholdProbability (k := k) G δ))

theorem threshold_parallel_repetition :
    ∃ κ₀ : ℝ, 0 < κ₀ ∧
      ∀ (x y a b : ℕ) (G : Game x y a b), entangledValue G < 1 →
      ∀ (δ : ℝ), 0 < δ → δ < 1 - entangledValue G →
      ∀ (k : ℕ), 1 ≤ k →
        (∀ S : RepeatedStrategy x y a b k,
          thresholdProbability G δ S ≤
            Real.exp (-κ₀ * δ ^ 13 / (1 + Real.log (((a + 1) * (b + 1) : ℕ) : ℝ)) * k)) ∧
        thresholdValue (k := k) G δ ≤
          Real.exp (-κ₀ * δ ^ 13 / (1 + Real.log (((a + 1) * (b + 1) : ℕ) : ℝ)) * k) := by
  sorry

end ThresholdParallelRepetition

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every finite two-player game with entangled value <i>v</i> &lt; 1, we prove exponential decay for the probability of winning at least a $`v+\delta`$ fraction of <i>k</i> independent repetitions, uniformly over all finite-dimensional joint strategies. The result allows arbitrary correlated question distributions and holds for every $`0\lt \delta\lt 1-v`$ and <i>k</i> ≥ 1. Its universal rate is proportional to $`\delta^5/(1+\log(|\mathcal A||\mathcal B|))`$, where $`\mathcal A,\mathcal B`$ are the answer alphabets. For each fixed question distribution, we also obtain an explicit cubic rate in <i>δ</i>.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
